<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:482px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg/480px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg.png" decoding="async" width="480" height="277" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg/720px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg/960px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1474" data-file-height="850" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_NIC_present.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Nicaragua map of Köppen climate classification</div></div></div>
<p>Nicaragua occupies a landmass of 130,967&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (50,567&#160;sq&#160;mi), which makes it slightly larger than England. Nicaragua has three distinct geographical regions: the Pacific lowlands – fertile valleys which the Spanish colonists settled, the <a href="/wiki/Amerrisque_Mountains" title="Amerrisque Mountains">Amerrisque Mountains</a> (North-central highlands), and the Mosquito Coast (Atlantic lowlands/<a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Lowlands" title="Caribbean Lowlands">Caribbean lowlands</a>).
</p><p>The low plains of the Atlantic Coast are 97&#160;km (60&#160;mi) wide in areas. They have long been exploited for their natural resources.
</p><p>On the Pacific side of Nicaragua are the two largest fresh water lakes in Central America—<a href="/wiki/Lake_Managua" title="Lake Managua">Lake Managua</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lake_Nicaragua" title="Lake Nicaragua">Lake Nicaragua</a>. Surrounding these lakes and extending to their northwest along the <a href="/wiki/Rift_valley" title="Rift valley">rift valley</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Fonseca" title="Gulf of Fonseca">Gulf of Fonseca</a> are fertile lowland plains, with soil highly enriched by <a href="/wiki/Volcanic_ash" title="Volcanic ash">ash</a> from nearby <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcanoes</a> of the central highlands. Nicaragua's abundance of biologically significant and unique <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystems</a> contribute to <a href="/wiki/Mesoamerica" title="Mesoamerica">Mesoamerica</a>'s designation as a <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot" title="Biodiversity hotspot">biodiversity hotspot</a>. Nicaragua has made efforts to become less dependent on fossil fuels, and it expects to acquire 90% of its energy from renewable resources by 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Nicaragua was one of the few countries that did not enter an <a href="/wiki/Intended_Nationally_Determined_Contributions" class="mw-redirect" title="Intended Nationally Determined Contributions">INDC</a> at <a href="/wiki/COP21" class="mw-redirect" title="COP21">COP21</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Nicaragua initially chose not to join the Paris Climate Accord because it felt that "much more action is required" by individual countries on restricting global temperature rise.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> However, in October 2017, Nicaragua made the decision to join the agreement.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> It ratified this agreement on November 22, 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nearly one fifth of Nicaragua is designated as <a href="/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Nicaragua" title="Protected areas of Nicaragua">protected areas</a> like national parks, nature reserves, and biological reserves. The country had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 3.63/10, ranking it 146th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Geophysical" class="mw-redirect" title="Geophysical">Geophysically</a>, Nicaragua is surrounded by the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Plate" title="Caribbean Plate">Caribbean Plate</a>, an <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_crust" title="Oceanic crust">oceanic</a> <a href="/wiki/Tectonic_plate" class="mw-redirect" title="Tectonic plate">tectonic plate</a> underlying Central America and the <a href="/wiki/Cocos_Plate" title="Cocos Plate">Cocos Plate</a>. Since Central America is a major <a href="/wiki/Subduction" title="Subduction">subduction</a> zone, Nicaragua hosts most of the <a href="/wiki/Central_American_Volcanic_Arc" class="mw-redirect" title="Central American Volcanic Arc">Central American Volcanic Arc</a>. On 9 June 2021, Nicaragua launched a new volcanic supersite research in strengthening the monitoring and surveillance of the country's 21 active volcanoes.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pacific_lowlands">Pacific lowlands</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Pacific lowlands">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_(landscape).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_%28landscape%29.jpg/220px-Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_%28landscape%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_%28landscape%29.jpg/330px-Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_%28landscape%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_%28landscape%29.jpg/440px-Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_%28landscape%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Concepci%C3%B3n_from_Maderas_(landscape).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Nicaragua is known as "the land of lakes and volcanoes"; pictured is <a href="/wiki/Concepci%C3%B3n_(volcano)" title="Concepción (volcano)">Concepción volcano</a>, as seen from <a href="/wiki/Maderas" title="Maderas">Maderas volcano</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>In the west of the country, these lowlands consist of a broad, hot, fertile plain. Punctuating this plain are several large volcanoes of the <a href="/wiki/Cordillera_Los_Maribios" class="mw-redirect" title="Cordillera Los Maribios">Cordillera Los Maribios</a> mountain range, including <a href="/wiki/Mombacho" title="Mombacho">Mombacho</a> just outside Granada, and <a href="/wiki/Momotombo" title="Momotombo">Momotombo</a> near León. The lowland area runs from the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Fonseca" title="Gulf of Fonseca">Gulf of Fonseca</a> to Nicaragua's Pacific border with Costa Rica south of <a href="/wiki/Lake_Nicaragua" title="Lake Nicaragua">Lake Nicaragua</a>. Lake Nicaragua is the largest freshwater lake in Central America (20th largest in the world),<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> and is home to some of the world's rare freshwater sharks (<a href="/wiki/Bull_shark" title="Bull shark">Nicaraguan shark</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The Pacific lowlands region is the most populous, with over half of the nation's population.
</p><p>The eruptions of western Nicaragua's 40 volcanoes, many of which are still active, have sometimes devastated settlements but also have enriched the land with layers of fertile ash. The geologic activity that produces vulcanism also breeds powerful earthquakes. Tremors occur regularly throughout the Pacific zone, and earthquakes have nearly destroyed the capital city, Managua, more than once.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:PenasBlancas,_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve,_Jinotega_Department,_Nicaragua.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/PenasBlancas%2C_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve%2C_Jinotega_Department%2C_Nicaragua.jpg/220px-PenasBlancas%2C_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve%2C_Jinotega_Department%2C_Nicaragua.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/PenasBlancas%2C_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve%2C_Jinotega_Department%2C_Nicaragua.jpg/330px-PenasBlancas%2C_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve%2C_Jinotega_Department%2C_Nicaragua.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/PenasBlancas%2C_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve%2C_Jinotega_Department%2C_Nicaragua.jpg/440px-PenasBlancas%2C_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve%2C_Jinotega_Department%2C_Nicaragua.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:PenasBlancas,_part_of_the_Bosawas_Reserve,_Jinotega_Department,_Nicaragua.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Peñas Blancas, part of the <a href="/wiki/Bosaw%C3%A1s_Biosphere_Reserve" title="Bosawás Biosphere Reserve">Bosawás Biosphere Reserve</a> is the second largest rainforest in the Western Hemisphere, after the Amazonian Rainforest in Brazil. Located northeast of the city of <a href="/wiki/Jinotega" title="Jinotega">Jinotega</a> in Northeastern Nicaragua.</div></div></div>
<p>Most of the Pacific zone is <i><a href="/wiki/Tierra_caliente" title="Tierra caliente">tierra caliente</a></i>, the "hot land" of tropical Spanish America at elevations under 610 metres (2,000&#160;ft). Temperatures remain virtually constant throughout the year, with highs ranging between 29.4 and 32.2&#160;°C (85 and 90&#160;°F). After a dry season lasting from November to April, rains begin in May and continue to October, giving the Pacific lowlands 1,016 to 1,524 millimetres (40 to 60&#160;in) of precipitation. Good soils and a favourable climate combine to make western Nicaragua the country's economic and demographic centre. The southwestern shore of Lake Nicaragua lies within 24 kilometres (15&#160;mi) of the Pacific Ocean. Thus the lake and the San Juan River were often proposed in the 19th century as the longest part of a canal route across the Central American isthmus. Canal proposals were periodically revived in the 20th and 21st centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Roughly a century after the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Panama_Canal" title="Panama Canal">Panama Canal</a>, the prospect of a Nicaraguan <a href="/wiki/Ecocanal" title="Ecocanal">ecocanal</a> remains a topic of interest.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In addition to its beach and resort communities, the Pacific lowlands contains most of Nicaragua's Spanish colonial architecture and artifacts. Cities such as León and <a href="/wiki/Granada,_Nicaragua" title="Granada, Nicaragua">Granada</a> abound in colonial architecture; founded in 1524, Granada is the oldest colonial city in the Americas.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="North_central_highlands">North central highlands</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: North central highlands">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG/220px-Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG/330px-Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG/440px-Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ca%C3%B1onsomoto.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Somoto_Canyon_National_Monument" title="Somoto Canyon National Monument">Somoto Canyon National Monument</a> is located in <a href="/wiki/Somoto" class="mw-redirect" title="Somoto">Somoto</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Madriz_Department" title="Madriz Department">Madriz Department</a> in Northern Nicaragua.</div></div></div>
<p>Northern Nicaragua is the most diversified region producing coffee, cattle, milk products, vegetables, wood, gold, and flowers. Its extensive forests, rivers and geography are suited for ecotourism.
</p><p>The central highlands are a significantly less populated and economically developed area in the north, between Lake Nicaragua and the Caribbean. Forming the country's <a href="/wiki/Tierra_templada" title="Tierra templada">tierra templada</a>, or "temperate land", at elevations between 610 and 1,524 metres (2,000 and 5,000&#160;ft), the highlands enjoy mild temperatures with daily highs of 23.9 to 26.7&#160;°C (75 to 80&#160;°F). This region has a longer, wetter rainy season than the Pacific lowlands, making erosion a problem on its steep slopes. Rugged terrain, poor soils, and low population density characterize the area as a whole, but the northwestern valleys are fertile and well settled.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The area has a cooler climate than the Pacific lowlands. About a quarter of the country's agriculture takes place in this region, with coffee grown on the higher slopes. <a href="/wiki/Oak" title="Oak">Oaks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pine" title="Pine">pines</a>, <a href="/wiki/Moss" title="Moss">moss</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fern" title="Fern">ferns</a> and <a href="/wiki/Orchidaceae" class="mw-redirect" title="Orchidaceae">orchids</a> are abundant in the <a href="/wiki/Cloud_forest" title="Cloud forest">cloud forests</a> of the region.
</p><p>Bird life in the forests of the central region includes <a href="/wiki/Resplendent_quetzal" title="Resplendent quetzal">resplendent quetzals</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lesser_goldfinch" title="Lesser goldfinch">goldfinches</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hummingbird" title="Hummingbird">hummingbirds</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jay" title="Jay">jays</a> and <a href="/wiki/Emerald_toucanet" title="Emerald toucanet">toucanets</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Caribbean_lowlands">Caribbean lowlands</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Caribbean lowlands">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>This large <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforest</a> region is irrigated by several large rivers and is sparsely populated. The area has 57% of the territory of the nation and most of its mineral resources. It has been heavily exploited, but much natural diversity remains. The <a href="/wiki/Coco_River" title="Coco River">Rio Coco</a> is the largest river in Central America; it forms the border with Honduras. The Caribbean coastline is much more sinuous than its generally straight Pacific counterpart; lagoons and deltas make it very irregular.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2014)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Nicaragua's <a href="/wiki/Bosaw%C3%A1s_Biosphere_Reserve" title="Bosawás Biosphere Reserve">Bosawás Biosphere Reserve</a> is in the Atlantic lowlands, part of which is located in the municipality of <a href="/wiki/Siuna" title="Siuna">Siuna</a>; it protects 7,300 square kilometres (1,800,000 acres) of <a href="/w/index.php?title=La_Mosquitia_(Nicaragua)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="La Mosquitia (Nicaragua) (page does not exist)">La Mosquitia</a> forest – almost 7% of the country's area – making it the largest rainforest north of the <a href="/wiki/Amazon_rainforest" title="Amazon rainforest">Amazon</a> in Brazil.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The municipalities of <a href="/wiki/Siuna" title="Siuna">Siuna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rosita,_Nicaragua" title="Rosita, Nicaragua">Rosita</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bonanza,_Nicaragua" title="Bonanza, Nicaragua">Bonanza</a>, known as the "Mining Triangle", are located in the region known as the <a href="/wiki/North_Caribbean_Coast_Autonomous_Region" title="North Caribbean Coast Autonomous Region">North Caribbean Coast Autonomous Region</a>, in the Caribbean lowlands. Bonanza still contains an active gold mine owned by HEMCO. Siuna and Rosita do not have active mines but panning for gold is still very common in the region.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2014)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Nicaragua's tropical east coast is very different from the rest of the country. The climate is predominantly tropical, with high temperature and high humidity. Around the area's principal city of Bluefields, English is widely spoken along with the official Spanish. The population more closely resembles that found in many typical Caribbean ports than the rest of Nicaragua.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A great variety of birds can be observed including <a href="/wiki/Eagle" title="Eagle">eagles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Toucan" title="Toucan">toucans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Parakeet" title="Parakeet">parakeets</a> and <a href="/wiki/Macaw" title="Macaw">macaws</a>. Other animal life in the area includes different species of <a href="/wiki/Monkey" title="Monkey">monkeys</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anteater" title="Anteater">anteaters</a>, white-tailed <a href="/wiki/Deer" title="Deer">deer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tapir" title="Tapir">tapirs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_and_fauna">Flora and fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Flora and fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Fauna_of_Nicaragua" title="Fauna of Nicaragua">Fauna of Nicaragua</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg/220px-Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg/330px-Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg/440px-Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1867" data-file-height="1334" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Turquoise-browed_Motmot_2495425451.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Guardabarranco" class="mw-redirect" title="Guardabarranco">Guardabarranco</a> ("ravine-guard") is Nicaragua's national bird.</div></div></div>
<p>Nicaragua is home to a rich variety of plants and animals. Nicaragua is located in the middle of the Americas and this privileged location has enabled the country to serve as host to a great biodiversity. This factor, along with the weather and light altitudinal variations, allows the country to harbor 248 species of amphibians and reptiles, 183 species of mammals, 705 bird species, 640 fish species, and about 5,796 species of plants.
</p><p>The region of great forests is located on the eastern side of the country. Rainforests are found in the <a href="/wiki/R%C3%ADo_San_Juan_Department" title="Río San Juan Department">Río San Juan Department</a> and in the autonomous regions of <a href="/wiki/North_Caribbean_Coast_Autonomous_Region" title="North Caribbean Coast Autonomous Region">RAAN</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Caribbean_Coast_Autonomous_Region" title="South Caribbean Coast Autonomous Region">RAAS</a>. This biome groups together the greatest biodiversity in the country and is largely protected by the <a href="/wiki/Indio_Ma%C3%ADz_Biological_Reserve" title="Indio Maíz Biological Reserve">Indio Maíz Biological Reserve</a> in the south and the <a href="/wiki/Bosaw%C3%A1s_Biosphere_Reserve" title="Bosawás Biosphere Reserve">Bosawás Biosphere Reserve</a> in the north. The Nicaraguan jungles, which represent about 9,700 square kilometres (2.4&#160;million acres), are considered the lungs of Central America and comprise the second largest-sized rainforest of the Americas.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are currently 78 protected areas in Nicaragua, covering more than 22,000 square kilometres (8,500&#160;sq&#160;mi), or about 17% of its landmass. These include <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_refuge" class="mw-redirect" title="Wildlife refuge">wildlife refuges</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nature_reserve" title="Nature reserve">nature reserves</a> that shelter a wide range of <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystems</a>. There are more than 1,400 animal species classified thus far in Nicaragua. Some 12,000 species of plants have been <a href="/wiki/Biological_classification" class="mw-redirect" title="Biological classification">classified</a> thus far in Nicaragua, with an estimated 5,000 species not yet classified.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Bull_shark" title="Bull shark">bull shark</a> is a species of shark that can survive for an extended period of time in fresh water. It can be found in <a href="/wiki/Lake_Nicaragua" title="Lake Nicaragua">Lake Nicaragua</a> and the <a href="/wiki/San_Juan_River_(Nicaragua)" title="San Juan River (Nicaragua)">San Juan River</a>, where it is often referred to as the "Nicaragua shark".<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> Nicaragua has recently banned freshwater fishing of the Nicaragua shark and the <a href="/wiki/Sawfish" title="Sawfish">sawfish</a> in response to the declining populations of these animals.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Nicaragua" title="Politics of Nicaragua">Politics of Nicaragua</a></div>

<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg/220px-Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg/330px-Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg/440px-Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dmitry_Medvedev_18_December_2008-6.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Nicaraguan president, <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Ortega" title="Daniel Ortega">Daniel Ortega</a> with then <a href="/wiki/Russian_President" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian President">Russian President</a> <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev" title="Dmitry Medvedev">Dmitry Medvedev</a> in Moscow in 2008</div></div></div>
<p>Politics of Nicaragua takes place in a framework of a <a href="/wiki/Presidential_system" title="Presidential system">presidential</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> republic, whereby the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Nicaragua" title="President of Nicaragua">President of Nicaragua</a> is both <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, and of a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>. <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">Executive power</a> is exercised by the government. <a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">Legislative power</a> is vested in both the government and the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Nicaragua" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of Nicaragua">national assembly</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judiciary</a> makes up the third branch of government.
</p><p>Between 2007 and 2009, Nicaragua's major political parties discussed the possibility of going from a presidential system to a parliamentary system. Their reason: there would be a clear differentiation between the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a> (prime minister) and the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> (president). Nevertheless, it was argued that the true reason for this proposal was to find a legal way for President Ortega to stay in power after January 2012, when his second and last government period was expected to end. Ortega was <a href="/wiki/2016_Nicaraguan_general_election" title="2016 Nicaraguan general election">reelected to a third term in November 2016</a>, and <a href="/wiki/2021_Nicaraguan_general_election" title="2021 Nicaraguan general election">a fourth in 2021</a>; both elections were tainted by credible reports of large-scale fraud, voter intimidation, and politically motivated arrests of opposition party leaders. Independent observers were barred from the polls. The <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_American_States" title="Organization of American States">OAS</a>, United States, and European Union all described the 2021 election as a "sham" due to these issues.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Ortega" title="Daniel Ortega">Daniel Ortega</a>'s election in 2006, liberal democratic norms and individual rights in practice have deteriorated. Parties other than the ruling <a href="/wiki/Sandinista_National_Liberation_Front" title="Sandinista National Liberation Front">FSLN</a> have been repressed through arbitrary arrest and detention of opposition candidates and activists. Most government jobs <i>de facto</i> require membership in the FSLN. Opposition media has been repressed through arrests of journalists and seizure of broadcasting and printing materials.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Nicaragua" title="Foreign relations of Nicaragua">Foreign relations of Nicaragua</a></div>
<p>Nicaragua pursues an independent foreign policy. <a href="/wiki/Territorial_disputes_of_Nicaragua" title="Territorial disputes of Nicaragua">Nicaragua is in territorial disputes</a> with Colombia over the <a href="/wiki/Archipelago_de_San_Andr%C3%A9s_y_Providencia" class="mw-redirect" title="Archipelago de San Andrés y Providencia">Archipelago de San Andrés y Providencia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Quita_Sue%C3%B1o_Bank" title="Quita Sueño Bank">Quita Sueño Bank</a> and with Costa Rica over a boundary dispute involving the <a href="/wiki/San_Juan_River_(Nicaragua)" title="San Juan River (Nicaragua)">San Juan River</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Armed_Forces" title="Nicaraguan Armed Forces">Nicaraguan Armed Forces</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Afghan MI-17 and An-26" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg/220px-Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg/330px-Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg/440px-Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1680" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Afghan_MI-17_and_AN-26.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/An-26" class="mw-redirect" title="An-26">AN-26</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mil_Mi-17" title="Mil Mi-17">Mi-17</a> are used by the <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Air_Force" title="Nicaraguan Air Force">Nicaraguan Air Force</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Armed_Forces" title="Nicaraguan Armed Forces">Nicaraguan Armed Forces</a> consist of various military contingents. Nicaragua has an <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Army" class="mw-redirect" title="Nicaraguan Army">army</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Navy" title="Nicaraguan Navy">navy</a> and an <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Air_Force" title="Nicaraguan Air Force">air force</a>. There are roughly 14,000 active duty personnel, which is much less compared to the numbers seen during the <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_Revolution" title="Nicaraguan Revolution">Nicaraguan Revolution</a>. Although the army has had a rough military history, a portion of its forces, which were known as the <a href="/wiki/National_Guard_(Nicaragua)" title="National Guard (Nicaragua)">national guard</a>, became integrated with what is now the <a href="/wiki/Polic%C3%ADa_Nacional_(Nicaragua)" class="mw-redirect" title="Policía Nacional (Nicaragua)">National Police of Nicaragua</a>. In essence, the police became a <i><a href="/wiki/Gendarmerie" title="Gendarmerie">gendarmerie</a></i>. The National Police of Nicaragua are rarely, if ever, labeled as a <i><a href="/wiki/Gendarmerie" title="Gendarmerie">gendarmerie</a></i>. The other elements and manpower that were not devoted to the national police were sent over to cultivate the new Army of Nicaragua.
</p><p>The age to serve in the armed forces is 17 and <a href="/wiki/Conscription" title="Conscription">conscription</a> is not imminent. As of 2006<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the military budget was roughly 0.7% of Nicaragua's expenditures.
</p><p>In 2017, Nicaragua signed the UN <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law_enforcement">Law enforcement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Law enforcement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Nicaragua" title="Law enforcement in Nicaragua">Law enforcement in Nicaragua</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2,photo01.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2%2Cphoto01.jpg/220px-600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2%2Cphoto01.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2%2Cphoto01.jpg/330px-600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2%2Cphoto01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2%2Cphoto01.jpg/440px-600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2%2Cphoto01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="400" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:600x400_1329442889_170212amb-nota2,photo01.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/National_Police_of_Nicaragua" title="National Police of Nicaragua">National Police of Nicaragua</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/National_Police_of_Nicaragua" title="National Police of Nicaragua">National Police of Nicaragua</a> Force (in Spanish: La Policía Nacional Nicaragüense) is the national police of Nicaragua. The force is in charge of regular police functions and, at times, works in conjunction with the Nicaraguan military, making it an indirect and rather subtle version of a gendarmerie.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2015)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> However, the Nicaraguan National Police work separately and have a different established set of norms than the nation's military.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2015)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> According to a recent US Department of State report, corruption is endemic, especially within law enforcement and the judiciary, and arbitrary arrests, torture, and harsh prison conditions are the norm.<sup id="cite_ref-Nicaragua_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nicaragua-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nicaragua is the safest country in Central America and one of the safest in Latin America, according to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Program" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Development Program">United Nations Development Program</a>, with a homicide rate of 8.7 per 100,000 inhabitants.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Departments_of_Nicaragua" title="Departments of Nicaragua">Departments of Nicaragua</a></div>
<p>Nicaragua is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary</a> republic. For administrative purposes it is divided into 15 <a href="/wiki/Department_(administrative_division)" title="Department (administrative division)">departments</a> (<i>departamentos</i>) and two self-governing regions (autonomous communities) based on the Spanish model. The departments are then <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Nicaragua" title="Municipalities of Nicaragua">subdivided into 153 municipios</a> (municipalities). The two autonomous regions are the North Caribbean Coast Autonomous Region
and South Caribbean Coast Autonomous Region, often referred to as RACCN and RACCS, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<td><b>16</b></td>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Nicaragua" title="Economy of Nicaragua">Economy of Nicaragua</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Nicaragua_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Nicaragua_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Nicaragua_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Nicaragua_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Nicaragua_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Nicaragua_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Nicaragua_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Nicaragua_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Nicaragua exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_in_Nicaragua.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Historical GDP per capita development of Nicaragua</div></div></div>
<p>Nicaragua is among the poorest countries in the Americas.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Its gross domestic product (GDP) in <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">purchasing power parity</a> (PPP) in 2008 was estimated at US$17.37&#160;billion.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Agriculture represents 15.5% of GDP, the highest percentage in Central America.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Remittances account for over 15% of the Nicaraguan GDP. Close to one billion dollars are sent to the country by Nicaraguans living abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> The economy grew at a rate of about 4% in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> By 2019, given restrictive taxes and a civil conflict, it recorded a negative growth of - 3.9%; the International Monetary Fund forecast for 2020 is a further decline of 6% due to COVID-19.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The restrictive tax measures put in place in 2019 and a political crisis over social security negatively affected the country's weak public spending and investor confidence in sovereign debt. According to the update IMF forecasts from 14 April 2020, due to the outbreak of the COVID-19, GDP growth is expected to fall to -6% in 2020.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The date of the event predicted near this tag has passed. (April 2021)">needs update</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>According to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme" title="United Nations Development Programme">United Nations Development Programme</a>, 48% of the population of Nicaragua live below the poverty line,<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> 79.9% of the population live with less than $2 per day,<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> According to UN figures, 80% of the <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples" title="Indigenous peoples">indigenous people</a> (who make up 5% of the population) live on less than $1 per day.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the World Bank, Nicaragua ranked as the 123rd out of 190 best economy for starting a business.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> In 2007, Nicaragua's economy was labelled "62.7% free" by the conservative <a href="/wiki/Think_tank" title="Think tank">think tank</a> <a href="/wiki/The_Heritage_Foundation" title="The Heritage Foundation">Heritage Foundation</a>, with high levels of fiscal, government, labor, investment, financial, and trade freedom.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> It ranked as the 61st <a href="/wiki/Index_of_Economic_Freedom" title="Index of Economic Freedom">freest economy</a>, and 14th (of 29) in the Americas.
</p><p>In March 2007, Poland and Nicaragua signed an agreement to write off 30.6&#160;million dollars which was borrowed by the Nicaraguan government in the 1980s.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> Inflation reduced from 33,500% in 1988 to 9.45% in 2006, and the foreign debt was cut in half.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:FruitColors.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/FruitColors.jpg/220px-FruitColors.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/FruitColors.jpg/330px-FruitColors.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/FruitColors.jpg/440px-FruitColors.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:FruitColors.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Coffee" title="Coffee">Coffee</a> is one of the most important exports of Nicaragua. It is grown in <a href="/wiki/Jinotega" title="Jinotega">Jinotega</a>, <a href="/wiki/Esteli" class="mw-redirect" title="Esteli">Esteli</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nueva_Segovia_Department" title="Nueva Segovia Department">Nueva Segovia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Matagalpa" title="Matagalpa">Matagalpa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Madriz_Department" title="Madriz Department">Madriz</a>, and exported worldwide through North America, <a href="/wiki/Latin_America" title="Latin America">Latin America</a>, Europe, Asia and Australia. Many coffee companies, like <a href="/wiki/Nestl%C3%A9" title="Nestlé">Nestlé</a> and <a href="/wiki/Starbucks" title="Starbucks">Starbucks</a>, buy Nicaraguan coffee.</div></div></div>
<p>Nicaragua is primarily an agricultural country; agriculture constitutes 60% of its total exports which annually yield approximately US$300&#160;million.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> Nearly two-thirds of the coffee crop comes from the northern part of the central highlands, in the area north and east of the town of Estelí.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Tobacco, grown in the same northern highlands region as coffee, has become an increasingly important cash crop since the 1990s, with annual exports of leaf and cigars in the neighborhood of $200&#160;million per year.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> Soil erosion and pollution from the heavy use of pesticides have become serious concerns in the cotton district. Yields and exports have both been declining since 1985.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Today most of Nicaragua's bananas are grown in the northwestern part of the country near the port of Corinto; sugarcane is also grown in the same district.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">Cassava</a>, a root crop somewhat similar to the potato, is an important food in tropical regions. Cassava is also the main ingredient in tapioca pudding.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Nicaragua's agricultural sector has benefited because of the country's strong ties to <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>. It is estimated that Venezuela will import approximately $200&#160;million in agricultural goods.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> In the 1990s, the government initiated efforts to diversify agriculture. Some of the new export-oriented crops were peanuts, <a href="/wiki/Sesame" title="Sesame">sesame</a>, melons, and onions.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Fishing boats on the Caribbean side bring shrimp as well as lobsters into processing plants at Puerto Cabezas, Bluefields, and Laguna de Perlas.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> A turtle fishery thrived on the Caribbean coast before it collapsed from <a href="/wiki/Overexploitation" title="Overexploitation">overexploitation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mining is becoming a major industry in Nicaragua,<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> contributing less than 1% of gross domestic product (GDP). Restrictions are being placed on lumbering due to increased environmental concerns about destruction of the rain forests. But lumbering continues despite these obstacles; indeed, a single hardwood tree may be worth thousands of dollars.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the war between the US-backed Contras and the government of the <a href="/wiki/Sandinista_National_Liberation_Front" title="Sandinista National Liberation Front">Sandinistas</a> in the 1980s, much of the country's infrastructure was damaged or destroyed.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> Transportation throughout the nation is often inadequate. For example, it was until recently impossible to travel all the way by highway from Managua to the Caribbean coast. A new road between Nueva Guinea and Bluefields was completed in 2019 and allows regular bus service to the capital.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> The Centroamérica power plant on the <a href="/wiki/Tuma_River" title="Tuma River">Tuma River</a> in the Central highlands has been expanded, and other hydroelectric projects have been undertaken to help provide electricity to the nation's newer industries.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Nicaragua has long been considered as a possible site for a <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua_Canal" title="Nicaragua Canal">new canal</a> that could supplement the Panama Canal, connecting the Caribbean Sea (and therefore the Atlantic Ocean) with the Pacific Ocean.
</p><p>Nicaragua's minimum wage is among the lowest in the Americas and in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> Remittances are equivalent to roughly 15% of the country's gross domestic product.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_6-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Growth in the <i>maquila</i> sector slowed in the first decade of the 21st century with rising competition from Asian markets, particularly China.<sup id="cite_ref-gr1_143-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gr1-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Land is the traditional basis of wealth in Nicaragua, with great fortunes coming from the export of staples such as coffee, cotton, beef, and sugar. Almost all of the upper class and nearly a quarter of the middle class are substantial landowners.
</p><p>A 1985 government study classified 69.4 percent of the population as poor on the basis that they were unable to satisfy one or more of their basic needs in housing, sanitary services (water, sewage, and garbage collection), education, and employment. The defining standards for this study were very low; housing was considered substandard if it was constructed of discarded materials with dirt floors or if it was occupied by more than four persons per room.
</p><p>Rural workers are dependent on agricultural wage labor, especially in coffee and cotton. Only a small fraction hold permanent jobs. Most are migrants who follow crops during the harvest period and find other work during the off-season. The "lower" peasants are typically smallholders without sufficient land to sustain a family; they also join the harvest labor force. The "upper" peasants have sufficient resources to be economically independent. They produce enough surplus, beyond their personal needs, to allow them to participate in the national and world markets.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg/220px-Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg/330px-Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg/440px-Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rotonda_Ruben_dario.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The capital city <a href="/wiki/Managua" title="Managua">Managua</a> at night</div></div></div><p>The urban lower class is characterized by the informal sector of the economy. The informal sector consists of small-scale enterprises that utilize traditional technologies and operate outside the legal regime of labor protections and taxation. Workers in the informal sector are self-employed, unsalaried family workers or employees of small-enterprises, and they are generally poor.
</p><p>Nicaragua's informal sector workers include tinsmiths, mattress makers, seamstresses, bakers, shoemakers, and carpenters; people who take in laundry and ironing or prepare food for sale in the streets; and thousands of peddlers, owners of small businesses (often operating out of their own homes), and market stall operators. Some work alone, but others labor in the small talleres (workshops/factories) that are responsible for a large share of the country's industrial production. Because informal sector earnings are generally very low, few families can subsist on one income.<sup id="cite_ref-Nicaragua_–_SOCIETY_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nicaragua_–_SOCIETY-188">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Like most Latin American nations Nicaragua is also characterized by a very small upper-class, roughly 2% of the population, that is very wealthy and wields the political and economic power in the country that is not in the hands of foreign corporations and private industries. These families are oligarchical in nature and have ruled Nicaragua for generations and their wealth is politically and economically horizontally and vertically integrated.
</p><p>Nicaragua is currently a member of the <a href="/wiki/Bolivarian_Alliance_for_the_Americas" class="mw-redirect" title="Bolivarian Alliance for the Americas">Bolivarian Alliance for the Americas</a>, which is also known as ALBA. ALBA has proposed creating a new currency, the <a href="/wiki/SUCRE_(currency)" class="mw-redirect" title="SUCRE (currency)">Sucre</a>, for use among its members. In essence, this means that the Nicaraguan córdoba will be replaced with the Sucre. Other nations that will follow a similar pattern include: <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>, Ecuador, <a href="/wiki/Bolivia" title="Bolivia">Bolivia</a>, Honduras, <a href="/wiki/Cuba" title="Cuba">Cuba</a>, Saint Vincent and the Grenadines, <a href="/wiki/Dominica" title="Dominica">Dominica</a> and <a href="/wiki/Antigua_and_Barbuda" title="Antigua and Barbuda">Antigua and Barbuda</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nicaragua is considering construction of a canal linking the Atlantic to the Pacific Ocean, which President Daniel Ortega has said will give Nicaragua its "economic independence".<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> Scientists have raised concerns about environmental impacts, but the government has maintained that the canal will benefit the country by creating new jobs and potentially increasing its annual growth to an average of 8% per year.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> The project was scheduled to begin construction in December 2014,<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> however the <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua_Canal" title="Nicaragua Canal">Nicaragua Canal</a> has yet to be started.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Nicaragua" title="Tourism in Nicaragua">Tourism in Nicaragua</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg/220px-Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg/330px-Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg/440px-Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="720" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Crucero_en_San_Juan.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Royal_Caribbean_International" title="Royal Caribbean International">Royal Caribbean</a> <a href="/wiki/Cruise_ship" title="Cruise ship">Cruise ship</a> docked near the beach at <a href="/wiki/San_Juan_del_Sur" title="San Juan del Sur">San Juan del Sur</a> in Southern Nicaragua</div></div></div><p>
By 2006, tourism had become the second largest industry in Nicaragua.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Previously, tourism had grown about 70% nationwide during a period of 7 years, with rates of 10%–16% annually.<sup id="cite_ref-C2_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-C2-195">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> The increase and growth led to the income from tourism to rise more than 300% over a period of 10 years.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> The growth in tourism has also positively affected the agricultural, commercial, and finance industries, as well as the construction industry. President <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Ortega" title="Daniel Ortega">Daniel Ortega</a> has stated his intention to use tourism to combat poverty throughout the country.<sup id="cite_ref-Nica_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nica-197">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> The results for Nicaragua's tourism-driven economy have been significant, with the nation welcoming one million tourists in a calendar year for the first time in its history in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg/220px-HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg/330px-HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg/440px-HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1080" data-file-height="771" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:HuellasdeAcahualinca.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ancient_footprints_of_Acahualinca" title="Ancient footprints of Acahualinca">2,100-year-old human footprints</a> called "Huellas de Acahualinca" preserved in volcanic mud near <a href="/wiki/Lake_Managua" title="Lake Managua">Lake Managua</a></div></div></div>
<p>Every year about 60,000 U.S. citizens visit Nicaragua, primarily business people, tourists, and those visiting relatives.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> Some 5,300 people from the U.S. reside in Nicaragua. The majority of tourists who visit Nicaragua are from the U.S., Central or South America, and Europe. According to the Ministry of Tourism of Nicaragua (INTUR),<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> the colonial cities of <a href="/wiki/Le%C3%B3n,_Nicaragua" title="León, Nicaragua">León</a> and <a href="/wiki/Granada,_Nicaragua" title="Granada, Nicaragua">Granada</a> are the preferred spots for tourists. Also, the cities of <a href="/wiki/Masaya" title="Masaya">Masaya</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rivas,_Nicaragua" title="Rivas, Nicaragua">Rivas</a> and the likes of <a href="/wiki/San_Juan_del_Sur" title="San Juan del Sur">San Juan del Sur</a>, <a href="/wiki/El_Ostional" title="El Ostional">El Ostional</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Fortress_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Fortress of the Immaculate Conception">Fortress of the Immaculate Conception</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ometepe_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Ometepe Island">Ometepe Island</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Mombacho" title="Mombacho">Mombacho</a> volcano, and the <a href="/wiki/Corn_Islands" title="Corn Islands">Corn Islands</a> among other locations are the main tourist attractions. In addition, <a href="/wiki/Ecotourism" title="Ecotourism">ecotourism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Recreational_fishing" title="Recreational fishing">sport fishing</a> and <a href="/wiki/Surfing" title="Surfing">surfing</a> attract many tourists to Nicaragua.
</p><p>According to the <i>TV Noticias</i> news program, the main attractions in Nicaragua for tourists are the beaches, the scenic routes, the architecture of cities such as León and Granada, <a href="/wiki/Ecotourism" title="Ecotourism">ecotourism</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Agritourism" title="Agritourism">agritourism</a> particularly in northern Nicaragua.<sup id="cite_ref-C2_195-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-C2-195">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> As a result of increased tourism, Nicaragua has seen its <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign direct investment</a> increase by 79.1% from 2007 to 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Nicaragua is referred to as <i>"the land of lakes and volcanoes"</i> due to the number of lagoons and lakes, and the chain of volcanoes that runs from the north to the south along the country's Pacific side.<sup id="cite_ref-Brierley_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brierley-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Wallace_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wallace-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CNNTravel_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CNNTravel-202">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> Today, only 7 of the 50 volcanoes in Nicaragua are considered active. Many of these volcanoes offer some great possibilities for tourists with activities such as <a href="/wiki/Hiking" title="Hiking">hiking</a>, <a href="/wiki/Climbing" title="Climbing">climbing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Camping" title="Camping">camping</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Human_swimming" class="mw-redirect" title="Human swimming">swimming</a> in crater lakes.</p><div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:LagunaApoyo.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/LagunaApoyo.jpg/220px-LagunaApoyo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="85" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/LagunaApoyo.jpg/330px-LagunaApoyo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/LagunaApoyo.jpg/440px-LagunaApoyo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="789" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:LagunaApoyo.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Apoyo_Lagoon_Natural_Reserve" title="Apoyo Lagoon Natural Reserve">Apoyo Lagoon Natural Reserve</a> is a nature reserve located between the departments of <a href="/wiki/Masaya" title="Masaya">Masaya</a> and <a href="/wiki/Granada" title="Granada">Granada</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Apoyo_Lagoon_Natural_Reserve" title="Apoyo Lagoon Natural Reserve">Apoyo Lagoon Natural Reserve</a> was created by the eruption of the Apoyo Volcano about 23,000 years ago, which left a huge 7&#160;km-wide crater that gradually filled with water. It is surrounded by the old crater wall.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> The rim of the lagoon is lined with restaurants, many of which have <a href="/wiki/Kayaks" class="mw-redirect" title="Kayaks">kayaks</a> available. Besides exploring the forest around it, many water sports are practiced in the lagoon, most notably <a href="/wiki/Kayaking" title="Kayaking">kayaking</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sand skiing has become a popular attraction at the <a href="/wiki/Cerro_Negro" title="Cerro Negro">Cerro Negro</a> volcano in <a href="/wiki/Le%C3%B3n,_Nicaragua" title="León, Nicaragua">León</a>. Both dormant and active volcanoes can be climbed. Some of the most visited volcanoes include the <a href="/wiki/Masaya_Volcano" title="Masaya Volcano">Masaya Volcano</a>, <a href="/wiki/Momotombo" title="Momotombo">Momotombo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mombacho" title="Mombacho">Mombacho</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cosig%C3%BCina" title="Cosigüina">Cosigüina</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ometepe" title="Ometepe">Ometepe</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Maderas" title="Maderas">Maderas</a> and <a href="/wiki/Concepci%C3%B3n_(volcano)" title="Concepción (volcano)">Concepción</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Nicaraguasee03.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Nicaraguasee03.JPG/220px-Nicaraguasee03.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Nicaraguasee03.JPG/330px-Nicaraguasee03.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Nicaraguasee03.JPG/440px-Nicaraguasee03.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Nicaraguasee03.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Solentiname_Islands" title="Solentiname Islands">Solentiname Islands</a> are tropical islands located in <a href="/wiki/Lake_Nicaragua" title="Lake Nicaragua">Lake Nicaragua</a> which are home to 76 bird species and are a growing ecotourism destination.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Ecotourism" title="Ecotourism">Ecotourism</a> aims to be ecologically and socially conscious; it focuses on local culture, wilderness, and adventure. Nicaragua's ecotourism is growing with every passing year.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> It boasts a number of ecotourist tours and perfect places for adventurers. Nicaragua has three eco-regions (the Pacific, Central, and Atlantic) which contain volcanoes, tropical rainforests, and agricultural land.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> The majority of the eco-lodges and other environmentally-focused touristic destinations are found on Ometepe Island,<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> located in the middle of Lake Nicaragua just an hour's boat ride from Granada. While some are foreign-owned, others are owned by local families.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nicaragua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguans" title="Nicaraguans">Nicaraguans</a> and <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Nicaragua" title="Demographics of Nicaragua">Demographics of Nicaragua</a></div>
<table class="wikitable" style="float: right; margin-left: 10px">
